## October 25 C224 — bounded reviewed repair with a stopped outcome

**Source qualified; protected migration and execution have not yet run.** The
new commands require a separately qualified C224 controller and the matching
reviewed canonical scripts. Neither the installed old executable nor the C208
controller acquires these commands through this document.

This profile reuses the existing release-pair journal, escrow, release slots and
selection pointer. The dedicated finite controller owns an armed Job for its
entire process lifetime before it creates children or writes transaction state.
It qualifies the reviewed candidate through bounded local headless MCP, stops
the complete owned tree and records `QUALIFIED_STOPPED`. Legacy detached-serving
operations retain their outside-Job and post-release serving requirements.

Supported sequence:

1. Identify the exact reviewed published source commit and remote branch SHA.
   Record required tests, independent review and the actual CI observation.
   Missing CI is not a passing CI result. Retain the existing candidate's genuine
   acknowledged review, immutable snapshot, producer result and build attestation.
2. Build the controller separately from the installed canonical executable.
   Copy it to a new immutable-by-procedure artifact path; record source commit,
   profile, size and SHA-256. Check that hash before every invocation. The
   reviewed candidate remains its separately attested artifact.
3. Migrate the qualified fixed scripts through the established source-only
   canonical fast-forward procedure: require the expected existing branch/HEAD,
   clean tracked tree, known untracked bytes, unchanged runtime/history/binding
   files, and exact canonical EXE/sidecar and candidate/producer readbacks.
   Use `git merge --ff-only <verified-reviewed-commit>`. No helper-path override,
   source reset, binary copy or checksum rewrite is part of this step.
4. Qualify the actual checked-out canonical helpers with the relevant bounded
   fixture checks, including Windows PowerShell 5 and PowerShell 7. Capture their
   raw hashes for fresh preflight. This is helper qualification, not promotion.
5. Use the exact noncanonical controller with the canonical workspace as working
   directory. The following checks are read-only with respect to product state:

   ```powershell
   .\catdesk.ps1 diagnose
   & $controller operator release-pair result-stopped
   ```

6. Prepare the explicit stopped-only operation using the existing acknowledged
   review record and immutable candidate identity:

   ```powershell
   & $controller operator release-pair prepare-stopped --review-record-id <record-id> --build-path <reviewed-relative-path> --expected-sha256 <candidate-sha256>
   ```

   Preparation writes preflight/owner metadata. It is not a read-only diagnostic.
   Require `PREFLIGHT_READY`, `mode=QUALIFIED_STOPPED`, `profile=HEADLESS_LOCAL`,
   the expected candidate digest and a fresh confirmation token. The fixed script,
   recovery helper, interpreter and controller identities are bound into the
   operation. Foreign or ambiguous preflights must be preserved.
7. Confirm that exact token once within its returned `expiresAtUnix` lease
   (currently at most 120 seconds):

   ```powershell
   & $controller operator release-pair confirm-stopped --confirmation-token <fresh-token>
   ```

   Keep the caller monitored and bounded. The synchronous operation stages the
   verified pair, probes the exact canonical image/process/listener, checks
   unauthenticated 401 and authenticated 200 initialize/tools-list responses,
   drains the candidate/helper Jobs, and commits its distinct stopped receipt.
   It creates no detached serving owner and does not release an armed child Job.
8. Read the durable result. Successful confirm/result output has the stopped
   record under `result`; require its exact transaction/candidate and
   `result.state=QUALIFIED_STOPPED`. Verify canonical EXE SHA-256 equals both the
   attested candidate and recorded sidecar; read back the trusted release slot,
   generation, receipt chain and selection pointer. Verify no canonical listener
   or test-owned candidate/helper remains. Preserve the original escrow and any
   independently trusted previous release.
9. Perform installed startup, authenticated MCP, external connector and project
   integration tests as separately qualified operations. `HEADLESS_LOCAL`
   qualification does not establish ordinary native startup, Secure MCP ingress,
   previous-version rollback or unattended operation. Preserve stopped Wake and
   recurring-automation controls until their own owner/lifecycle gates pass.
10. Record the actual outcome, artifact hashes, checks, remaining gates and exact
    published SHA. A successful compilation or process start alone is insufficient.

For an interrupted claimed operation, inspect the durable result first. Preserve
all records and unknown bytes. After the exact prior controller/session and named
Jobs are proven quiescent, the explicit same-transaction continuation is:

```powershell
& $controller operator release-pair resume-stopped --transaction-id <existing-transaction>
```

Before receipt/slot publication, supported transaction rollback is:

```powershell
& $controller operator release-pair rollback-stopped --transaction-id <existing-transaction>
```

Rollback requires intact bound escrow, unchanged reviewed inputs, and genuine
whole-tree/empty-listener proof. It restores the recorded original tuple and
returns operator attention; that original tuple can remain divergent. After
receipt/slot publication use supported same-transaction completion, not rollback
over a possibly accepted version. Cross-session, missing-artifact, foreign-owner
or unknown-byte observations remain attention conditions. Never repeat confirm
after an ambiguous result, erase a claim or fabricate a job-release receipt.

### Unknown-rollover disposition — use exact state and qualified controller

Use a controller independently built and qualified from the revision containing
these commands. An older selected package is not assumed to support them.

```text
<qualified-controller> operator web rollover disposition-prepare --project-id <project> --expected-intent-id <quarantined-intent> --resolution-id <stable-resolution> --acknowledge-unknown ABANDONED_UNKNOWN_NO_REPLAY
<qualified-controller> operator web rollover disposition-confirm --project-id <project> --expected-intent-id <quarantined-intent> --resolution-id <same-resolution> --confirmation-token <returned-token> --acknowledge-unknown ABANDONED_UNKNOWN_NO_REPLAY
```

Prepare requires the exact unknown quarantine, unchanged paired source target,
completed source-task and handoff evidence, an available project writer/browser
lock, and no managed native binding, unresolved task journal, own-project work or
continuation. Own-project delivery responses must already be complete.
It returns `PREFLIGHT_READY` with pinned evidence and a ten-minute token.
Confirmation rechecks those gates, leaves the original `REQUESTED` context ledger
byte-for-byte unchanged, and records immutable disposition/completion receipts.
Those receipts and the exact saved intent gate admission. Changed evidence,
a busy owner or a previous continuation refuses. An expired unstarted preparation
requires a new resolution ID; an already-started exact transaction uses its existing recovery
path. Do not edit the token, journal or context files.

Success reports `ABANDONED_UNKNOWN_NO_REPLAY`: the provider outcome remains
**unknown**. It preserves original journal/handoff bytes, creates no provider
receipt, sends nothing and changes no binding. The old task/intent stays fenced.
New work still uses normal admission, and another rollover needs a fresh
completed task and distinct stable intent. This does not resolve arbitrary
drafts, authorize a resend or grant production-release authority.

Later completed work does not by itself prevent disposition. Preparation pins
the current own-project queue, archive, delivery and timer content; confirmation
must observe the same quiescent state. New work or changed content requires a
fresh preparation under a new resolution ID. A fresh rollover requires a
completed source event created after disposition, the exact saved predecessor
intent and the normal target/browser checks. It writes a new `REQUESTED` intent;
it never rewrites the old outcome as success or failure.

## October 18 draft recovery commands (source; live verification pending)


`inspect-draft` compares the complete normalized composer with the journal's
project, intent, predecessor and handoff. It returns bounded digests and status,
without typing, clearing or sending. An exact match proves draft ownership;
it does not prove an earlier submission never occurred.

`archive-draft` is restricted to a legacy `SUBMITTING` intent with no recorded
send boundary, receipt or replacement binding. It preserves the actual composer
text and expected journal text privately before permitting an exact-match
composer clear. A changed or unrelated draft is preserved. The old intent stays
non-replayable; the command never sends, rebinds a target or starts another task.

New protocol-2 rollovers persist `TEXT_ENTRY_AUTHORIZED`, `DRAFT_READY` and
`SEND_ATTEMPTED` before each browser acknowledgement. Automatic recovery of a
pre-send record requires its exact full saved draft. Missing, partial, changed
or ambiguous drafts require attention. `SEND_ATTEMPTED` and legacy unknown
records are never automatically replayed. Existing STOP, project ownership and
paired Wake binding checks still apply at every authorization boundary.

retains the existing isolated browser profile, Python runtime, queues and

protected installed runtime. The supported live update succeeded with


Use the private observed URL only in the local command input, not in a public

`start` requests a managed development host; `run` is foreground. Neither was
used in the local three-project fixture. Prepare requires an empty workspace;

```text
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web project register --project-id catdesk --workspace <exact-control-Git-root> --git-identity <exact-control-origin>
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web project register --project-id PROJECT --workspace <exact-project-Git-root> --git-identity <exact-project-origin>

```

With the development host still `STOPPED`, open the development browser for

```text
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web context capture --project-id PROJECT
```

The login command accepts no free conversation URL and uses the project's
paired registry/Wake target. Sign in in the visible browser, then press Enter
in the launching terminal so the command can close it. Successful browser
cleanup proves only that the window closed; it is not authentication,
target readiness, delivery or review evidence. Check the separate read-only
context capture result for the exact target before sending work. This route
does not operate the protected installed browser profile.

and does not edit the project checkout. Existing native registrations cannot
be replaced by this route. Bind each project to its own real, independent

Source defines central work and context controls in **SupervisorOnly Computer/Both**
MCP mode. Read `autonomy_project_registry_read` first and use
the chosen project's **current paired** Wake generation and URL SHA-256 for
`catdesk_web_work_request`:

```json
{"projectId":"PROJECT","requestId":"STABLE_REQUEST_ID","expectedTargetGeneration":1,"expectedTargetSha256":"<current-paired-64-hex-sha>","taskText":"Continue the assigned project work."}
```

The tool records one durable Wake work request for that project; same-ID,
same-target retries are idempotent, and another request waits while its work
intent is active.
`queue.workRequests` reports transport state without task text;
`workIntent.active` reports the single active intent. A queued receipt does not
prove browser delivery, assistant acknowledgement, or completed project work.
The tool does not take over a native Codex writer.

To close the exact request, call `catdesk_web_work_resolve` with that
request's **original** generation and URL SHA-256 from `workIntent`, including
when the current chat has rolled over:

```json
{"projectId":"PROJECT","requestId":"STABLE_REQUEST_ID","expectedTargetGeneration":1,"expectedTargetSha256":"<original-request-64-hex-sha>","outcome":"completed","summary":"Verified artifacts and remaining work."}
```

`completed` requires the exact Wake `SENT` receipt and completed response
timer. `retired` can close a known pre-submit request without a browser send;
submitted work still needs those proofs. Both outcomes are caller-reported,
not independent proof of task completion. The durable ledger retains prior
requests; other projects and existing Codex conversations continue separately.

the acceptance ledger records their exact scope. The earlier
`MANUAL_SIGN_IN_PENDING` state in session 94778 was superseded by its normal

### Per-project context policy and observations (source)

Use the **current paired** target from registry Web status for all context
tools, including after rollover. Policy is project-scoped and retained across
conversation generations. All three automatic flags must be explicit:

```json
{"projectId":"PROJECT","expectedTargetGeneration":1,"expectedTargetSha256":"<current-paired-64-hex-sha>","policy":{"configuredBudgetTokens":96000,"safetyHeadroomTokens":24000,"unknownSourceReserveTokens":8000,"automaticRolloverEnabled":false,"automaticObservationEnabled":false,"incompleteHistoryRollover":false}}
```

Pass this to `catdesk_web_context_configure`. The budget is a conservative local
setting, not a claim about the provider's context window. Automatic flags affect
later work by an already authorized running worker; this metadata operation
starts no browser or host.

To supplement the existing browser meter with one caller-reported observation,
use `catdesk_web_context_observe`:

```json
{"projectId":"PROJECT","expectedTargetGeneration":1,"expectedTargetSha256":"<current-paired-64-hex-sha>","event":{"eventId":"tool-result-ONE","revision":1,"dedupKey":"tool-result-ONE","sourceKind":"TOOL_RESULT","visibleText":"Bounded text actually visible in this project's conversation."}}
```

Text is limited to 65,536 UTF-8 bytes. Reuse event/dedup IDs for retries; increase
the revision when that same content changes. Same-revision conflicting content
is refused. Use distinct IDs for distinct messages. `UNKNOWN_ATTACHMENT` needs
`visibleText:null`; the configured reserve is advisory. Tool arguments are
tracked as traffic separately from visible message/tool-result occupancy.

The server supplies observation time and stores hashes/counts, never raw text.
Responses retain `originAuthenticated:false` and `CALLER_REPORTED` provenance;
explicit project scope does not authenticate the calling ChatGPT conversation.
Target and registration changes serialize with these mutations. A stale paired
witness refuses; a current replacement records a separate ledger and preserves
its predecessor. These controls publish no Wake event or native work request.
Installed/connector availability requires its own acceptance and catalog read.

`catdesk_web_context_capture` uses the existing browser snapshot owner to read
one visible excerpt and update the same count/hash ledger:

```json
{"projectId":"PROJECT","expectedTargetGeneration":1,"expectedTargetSha256":"<current-paired-64-hex-sha>"}
```

This explicit operation can open one temporary browser reader. It sends no
message, changes no binding and starts no recurring worker or WakeHost. It has
a fixed 180-second cooperative budget with existing bounded adapter cleanup. A
cancelled request keeps its project slot until its worker exits; duplicate
capture returns `WEB_CONTEXT_CAPTURE_BUSY`. Up to four captures may be active
in one server process, subject to the existing shared browser owner lock.
Sibling metadata/status operations do not wait for a browser snapshot.

The reader checks the expected generation/digest before reading and again
before ingestion; an intervening rebind refuses. Results contain counts with
`BOUND_BROWSER_SNAPSHOT` provenance and `browserBindingVerified:true`, while
`originAuthenticated:false` still applies to the caller. Visible excerpts retain
unknown-history coverage and are not exact provider context usage. Existing
owner/package checks can return `WEB_CONTEXT_CAPTURE_OWNER_BUSY` or
`WEB_CONTEXT_CAPTURE_PACKAGE_UNSUPPORTED`. Other backend details remain private.
The guarded reader has also been exercised through the existing explicit
standalone command:

```text
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web context capture --project-id PROJECT
```

It resolves the current paired target, then uses the same witnessed worker and
reports `invocationSource:STANDALONE_OPERATOR`. The earlier 60-second overall
limit preempted the adapter's existing 90-second startup allowance; the fixed
180-second budget includes its existing profile-lock/startup phases and the
bounded snapshot read. Internal limits and cleanup remain in place. This command
returns before TUI/MCP initialization and starts no recurring worker or WakeHost.
Keep those recurring starts stopped under the current operator instruction.
The actual central connector catalog remains unverified separately.

Online context configuration/observation and snapshot ingestion use nonblocking
ledger access. `WEB_CONTEXT_BUSY` means no waiting lock owner was displaced;
refresh status and retry the same stable event ID/revision after the writer
releases it. A snapshot ingestion refusal also releases that capture's worker
slot. Reusing IDs retains normal deduplication if an earlier metadata write
completed before a later read refused. Other explicit maintenance operations
retain their existing store mode. No busy response authorizes clearing locks,
resending Wake work or changing a conversation binding.

For a controlled event after an exact paired target exists:

```text
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web-dev-runtime enqueue --project-id PROJECT --event-id dev-UNIQUE_ID --message-file <workspace-contained-plain-text-file>
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web-dev-runtime retry --project-id PROJECT --event-id dev-EXISTING_ID
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web status
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web context configure --project-id PROJECT --input-file <policy-json>
<source-built-catdesk.exe> operator web auto tick --project-id PROJECT
```

The event ID must begin with `dev-`. The message file must be a normal file
inside the control workspace, outside `.catdesk` and `.git`; the normalized
outgoing message is capped at 2,048 bytes. Exact same-ID retries are
idempotent; changed content conflicts. A queued response reports
`deliveryProved:false` and `naturalReviewProved:false`; inspect actual Wake
delivery and the exact ChatGPT conversation before claiming either. Context
capture and rollover policies are configured per project. Automatic
observation is opt-in, and background browser work requires a running host.

Use `retry` only for an existing controlled development event whose exact
project and current paired target still match and whose Wake delivery is
`ATTENTION` before submission with no receipt. It moves that same event back
to `CLAIMED` through Wake's durable retry transition. It creates no new event
and does not prove a browser send. `SUBMITTING`, `SENT`, missing queue events,
and stale targets remain refused.

This is an unprotected development lane, not an installed release. The earlier
local fixture prepared no running host and performed no browser send; the
later live five-project deliveries are recorded above. They do not establish
completed rollover, protected Wake migration or exact remote CI acceptance.
Existing native project chats need not pause. See
`docs/OCTOBER_2026_MVP_ACCEPTANCE.md` for acceptance state.

## October 18 divergent canonical release-pair recovery (source; not installed acceptance)

From the control workspace, use a new independently reviewed executable that
contains this command and is **not** `target/release/catdesk.exe`:

```text
<reviewed-candidate.exe> operator release-pair prepare --review-record-id <id> --build-path <workspace-contained-reviewed-exe> --expected-sha256 <64-hex-sha>
<reviewed-candidate.exe> operator release-pair confirm --confirmation-token <returned-token>
<reviewed-candidate.exe> operator release-pair result
```

Prepare uses the existing independent review/snapshot/producer preflight,
then binds the actually divergent canonical binary and raw sidecar, preserved
operational slot-a, fixed scripts and interpreter. It does not invoke ordinary
promotion confirmation. The confirmation expires after 120 seconds. Confirm
requires a noncanonical worker image outside an enclosing Windows Job; a
canonical mapped process or occupied listener prevents staging. These checks
must pass before a release can be attempted.

The native worker owns the candidate and descendants until persistent handoff.
Only exact serving-owner proof after Job disarm and close can authorize
`reviewed_reconciliation` slot-b. Pre-handoff rollback requires whole-tree exit
and restores both original files byte-for-byte. The scripts do not launch the
daemon themselves. The external tunnel is not restarted.

`RESULT_UNAVAILABLE` means no result/claim is available. A valid claim without
a terminal result reports `CLAIMED_OUTCOME_UNPROVEN_OPERATOR_ATTENTION`; it
does not establish a live worker or permit replay. `RECONCILED_CANONICAL_READY`
is a historical transaction result, not a fresh current-health assertion.
Attention outcomes preserve evidence for inspection. Do not clear claims or
edit sidecar/slot metadata to retry.

The implementation and temporary fixtures are verified at source `2cf2751`.
No installed transaction has run; obtain actual independent candidate/release
acceptance before use. Source tests do not establish three-project Wake/Web
delivery or automatic rollover. See `docs/OCTOBER_2026_MVP_ACCEPTANCE.md`.

## October 17 Web observation and rollover driver (source; installed acceptance pending)

Use the central control workspace and a verified paired project/Wake target:

```text
catdesk operator web status
catdesk operator web context configure --project-id PROJECT --input-file POLICY_JSON
catdesk operator web auto tick --project-id PROJECT
catdesk operator web rollover status --project-id PROJECT
```

The policy adds `automaticObservationEnabled` (default false) for periodic
read-only context capture. `automaticRolloverEnabled` also enables observation.
The existing budget, headroom, unknown-source reserve and optional
`incompleteHistoryRollover` settings retain their meanings. Configure each
project independently; recent excerpts still cannot prove whole-chat coverage.

The production service and non-isolated headless MCP host drive eligible
projects every 120 seconds, with independent project workers and a shared
browser-session lease. A stopped or paused Wake host does not start browser
work. The driver captures context, requests and collects an exact completed
task handoff, submits a replacement, saves its observed URL and USER receipt,
then observes its answer before binding and queuing continuation. Central Web
status includes each project's durable scheduler and rollover state.

`RESPONSE_PENDING` means the replacement receipt is saved; a later tick only
observes that conversation. `SUBMITTING` without a receipt is an uncertain
outcome and never permits automatic resubmission. Intervening source work or
target divergence requires reconciliation with the saved receipt and queue
preserved. An exact saved Wake-first transition can finish its registry update
only while the handoff, native owner and destination still match. Legacy/manual
handoff files without a collected source witness report
`SOURCE_WITNESS_REQUIRED` to the driver; they remain explicit operator workflows.
These commands do not release or pause an existing native Codex
conversation. Installed package approval, target alignment and live acceptance
remain separate requirements; see `docs/OCTOBER_2026_MVP_ACCEPTANCE.md`.

A completed continuation can supply the original task for a later rollover
only when its saved handoff, native owner, destination, exact USER receipt and
completed response timer agree. A saved handoff request is published again
under the same event ID after a pre-publication interruption; it does not create
a second request or authorize resubmitting an uncertain browser turn.

## October 17 queue review invitation preparation and publication (source)

After local verification, review-package creation, and a live local disclosure grant, the operator can prepare an exact-package review request:

```text
catdesk.exe codex queue review-invite-prepare --project-id <id> --workspace <registered root> --receipt-id <uuid> --expected-package-sha256 <lowercase hash>
```

The command writes one create-once queue receipt sidecar with the package, project binding and registered Wake-compatible target digest. Every repeat rechecks current registration, package and live disclosure; expiry, revocation, tampering or target drift refuse readback. The preparation record remains `wakePublication=NOT_PUBLISHED`, `independentReview=PENDING`, and `wakeReady=false`. Preparation alone does not post an event or authorize another task.

Before the first immutable write, preparation now requires the installed Wake
owner, activation and paired project target, with its timestamp strictly after
the current generation's cutoff. If binding just changed, wait before preparing.
An older persisted invitation is preserved and requires a new receipt.

The source now exposes `catdesk_codex_queue_review_invitation_publish` in
SupervisorOnly Computer/Both MCP mode. Supply exactly `projectId`, `receiptId`
and `expectedPackageSha256` for that prepared invitation. The publisher
rechecks package bytes, the active local disclosure grant, project registration
and installed Wake authority. It durably records the target before queuing the
fixed review message. Retries retain the original event ID and target;
`OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` requires reconciliation and never retargets the request.
`WAKE_EVENT_ACCEPTED` proves queue acceptance only. Delivery, independent review,
acknowledgement and next-task authorization still need their own evidence.
Installed publication and three-project live acceptance remain unverified.

## October 16 source-only queue review disclosure renewal

If an exact queue package's one-hour local disclosure grant expires before ChatGPT finishes reading it, a human may renew that same grant in an interactive local terminal:

```text
catdesk.exe codex queue review-disclosure-renew --project-id <id> --workspace <registered root> --receipt-id <uuid> --expected-package-sha256 <lowercase hash>
```

The terminal requires `RENEW <uuid> <lowercase hash>`. Renewal is available in the last five minutes of the current window or after expiry. Each renewal starts a new one-hour window and writes a create-once, hash-linked history record; the original grant remains. The command rechecks the current project, target and immutable package under receipt, queue and registration locks. A revoked disclosure cannot be renewed. MCP reads revalidate the whole history and reject a changed package or grant. This remains shared-principal read access to untrusted review evidence; `independentReview=PENDING` and `wakeReady:false` remain unchanged. This source increment has no installed disclosure, independent ChatGPT decision, Wake event or CI acceptance.

```text
catdesk.exe codex queue operator-review-receipt --receipt-id <uuid>
```

The first command also accepts `REJECT` or `INCONCLUSIVE` and asks for `<DECISION> <uuid> <lowercase hash>` on the terminal. ## Rule zero: try CatDesk tools first

When the **CatDesk Local Tunnel v4** connector is `CONNECTED_VERIFIED` / `READY`, the assistant should execute supported project-local operations itself. Do not reflexively hand an operator a PowerShell command. First check transport, current status, available tools, and protected-state preconditions. If a narrow tool rejects an operation, check the **existing guarded operator path**; never bypass the policy by writing protected Wake/registry files directly.

**Source-of-truth order:** project registry + installed independent WakeHost readback, current session/review and Git evidence, then historical logs. A historical failure from an unrelated earlier chat is *forensic only*; use the latest applicable event and its exact receipt when evaluating whether current Wake works.

## 1. Rebind canonical CatDesk chat and independent Wake **together**

This is the preferred **assistant-side, protected/transactional rollover** (not a PowerShell command). It is backed by `operator_update_designated_chat_target` in `src/mcp.rs`; it compares the old digest, quarantines an existing `SUBMITTING` event on its old immutable target if needed, updates the Wake generation and the project registry in one guarded operation, and verifies the readback:

```text
CatDesk MCP: autonomy_project_registry_bind
  projectId:    catdesk
  decision:     DESIGNATED_CHAT_TARGET_URL=https://chatgpt.com/c/<NEW_CONVERSATION_UUID>
  expectedSha256: <CURRENT_64_HEX_DESIGNATED_TARGET_DIGEST>
```

**Obtain the current digest, do not reuse an old one:** call `autonomy_project_registry_read` and `catdesk_transport_status`. Require that project `catdesk`'s `chatgptTargetSha256` matches independent Wake `targets.catdesk.digest`. After the rollover require both to show exactly the new URL and digest and expect the Wake generation to increase by one. Do not retry blindly if compare-and-swap rejects.

**Why the ordinary target setter is not equivalent:** `catdesk_wake_target_set` updates only the project-local Wake target and is fail-closed when a prior Wake is `SUBMITTING`. The project-registry-only `autonomy_project_registry_chat_target_bind` does not perform the paired rollover. If a prior event is in flight, **use the designated chat rollover** above, not unpaired setters.

**Chat55 caution (2026-10-08):** Do not use `autonomy_project_registry_chat_target_bind` even with `action=PREFLIGHT` as a presumed read-only probe. A live call immediately updated the registry-only URL. Its return-to-old-URL operation restored the old URL but produced a registry SHA-256 that differs from the independent WakeHost-reported digest; the subsequent approved `DESIGNATED_CHAT_TARGET_URL` transaction was rejected with `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Until the existing guarded Binagotchy CLI `target set` or supported paired tool reports successful coherent readback, do not issue any manual Wake to the new chat or claim canonical migration. Preserve the old incomplete event, do not attempt unpaired repairs or write protected target files.

**Existing PowerShell CLI fallback** (**HISTORICAL EXAMPLE—DO NOT RUN WHILE OLD INSTALLED BINAGOTCHY MAY RESTART STOPPED WAKE**; use only after the stop-preservation patch is reviewed/installed and the guarded paired rollover is safe):

```powershell
Set-Location 'C:\Users\Volap\OneDrive\Desktop\Projects\CatDesk-codex-loop'; @('target set https://chatgpt.com/c/<NEW_CONVERSATION_UUID>','target','wake status','exit') | & '.\target\debug\catdesk.exe' --catdesk-binagotchy-cli
```

This invokes the *existing* Binagotchy `target set` implementation; the CLI calls the same guarded `operator_update_designated_chat_target` transaction. It must not be replaced with manual modification of `.catdesk` or independent Wake store files. `target` without `set` shows the current designated target. **Important:** An already-running Binagotchy console owns a singleton mutex. In that case the one-shot CLI can exit without applying the change. Close the existing Binagotchy console first, then run the line, and require an explicit `Designated Chat URL updated` plus independent project+Wake readback; absence of output does **not** establish success.

**Verified example (2026-10-08):** previously retained target generation 30 at `https://chatgpt.com/c/6ac63f72-a2ac-83e9-bf61-db8ab9d97224` was changed successfully through the MCP designated-chat route to generation 31 at `https://chatgpt.com/c/6ac6cbe8-6f0c-83ea-9f7d-13489d4d87f5`. Both WakeHost and registry independently reported digest `3a1d4cc69dfa3d05cb2bc33ef1197ac5a7433a120cfb4214a6996edb5a9d29e9`. That digest is **historical**, not a permanent default.

## 2. Wake operations (existing tools; no new setter required)

| Intent | Preferred CatDesk MCP operation | Binagotchy CLI command |
|---|---|---|
| Current Wake and receipt/status | `catdesk_transport_status`; `catdesk_binagotchy_command(status)` | `wake status` |
| Inspect current queue | `catdesk_binagotchy_command(queue)` | `wake queue` |
| Send **one** diagnostic manual Wake | `catdesk_binagotchy_command(test)` | `wake send` or `wake test` |
| Resume installed Wake | `catdesk_binagotchy_command(resume)` | `wake resume` |
| Start, pause or stop | `catdesk_binagotchy_command(start/pause/stop)` | `wake start`, `wake pause`, `wake stop` |
| Retire an *explicitly identified* stale event | `catdesk_binagotchy_command(retire, eventId=...)` | `wake retire <event-id>` |

Manual `test` events carry a **NOT natural acceptance** marker; a successful enqueue is not proof of actual delivery. Require exact current-event `EXACT_USER_MESSAGE_APPENDED` receipt and terminal state before declaring delivery passed. Ignore unrelated historical `ATTENTION`, `staleCount`, and old chats when assessing the latest Wake, but preserve their records for root-cause analysis. Do not blind-replay old events, nor retire records solely because they are old.

When source changes to Wake need installation, use the reviewed immutable installed-Wake procedure; do not arbitrarily restart, swap binaries, or mutate the external Secure MCP tunnel.

## 3. Turn timer on **every** substantial ChatGPT/CatDesk session

```text
CatDesk MCP: catdesk_turn_timer({action:"START", allow_without_plan:true})
CatDesk MCP: catdesk_turn_timer({action:"STATUS", timerId:"<returned-id>", allow_without_plan:true})
CatDesk MCP: catdesk_turn_timer({action:"STOP", timerId:"<returned-id>", allow_without_plan:true})
```

The independent timer runs to 20 minutes, with an 18-minute checkpoint; **aim to checkpoint/stop by 19 minutes**. Capture and use only the returned `timerId`. Stop or hand off before the lease expires, record durable state, and allow the hourly deadman to defer to any active writer. An expired historical timer is not authority to overwrite another session.

## 4. Routine lifecycle, diagnostics and recovery (PowerShell from workspace)

```powershell
.\catdesk.ps1 status
.\catdesk.ps1 diagnose
.\catdesk.ps1 recover
```

- `status`: nonmutating summary of the fixed canonical lifecycle.
- `diagnose`: nonmutating nine-layer diagnostic (`LIFECYCLE_ENGINE`, `CANONICAL_RELEASE`, `RECOVERY_AUTHORITY`, `LOCAL_MCP_CONFIG`, `LOCAL_DAEMON`, `LOCAL_MCP_PROTOCOL`, `WAKE_RUNTIME`, `OFFICIAL_RUNTIME`, `CODEX_CLI`), with primary failing layer and next action.
- `recover`: existing guarded recovery path. Use only after inspecting `diagnose` and relevant authority; do not treat recovery as a way around reviewed promotion or the Secure MCP tunnel ownership boundary.

Other facade commands currently accepted by `catdesk.ps1`: `install`, `start`, `stop`, `autostart`, `wake`. Check arguments and release authority before invoking mutating actions. Never interpret a healthy dev/debug `cargo build` as reviewed serving-release attestation.

## 5. Git / GitHub and Codex

```powershell
git status --short
git rev-parse HEAD
git rev-parse origin/orchestrator/chatgpt-codex-autonomous-loop
git diff --check
git pull --ff-only origin orchestrator/chatgpt-codex-autonomous-loop
```

Compare local/remote HEAD and do not force-push or modify `main`. Stage **only reviewed files**, e.g. `git add docs/orchestrator/CATDESK_IMPORTANT_COMMANDS.md`; never `git add -A` while unrelated untracked artifacts are present. Use the connected GitHub connector when direct Git operations are unavailable, then fast-forward local.

For **interactive Codex CLI session continuation**:

```powershell
codex resume --all
```

Select the intended existing session (use session directory when specifically prompted); then enter `/goal resume` **inside that session**. Usage availability and the user's cloud/local provider routing policy must be checked first; do not assume that invoking `codex` means capacity is available.

## 8. Python verification and Wake source/deployment separation

The supported project-local CatDesk `run_command` operation accepts narrow, bounded `pytest` commands with `allow_without_plan=true`; use it when `verify_project` has a broad-suite timeout. Useful commands:

```powershell
pytest --collect-only -q
pytest tests/test_stable_wake_browser_adapter.py -q
pytest tests/advisors/test_deepseek_web_advisor.py -q
pytest tests/test_wake_bridge.py -k receipt -q
pytest tests/test_wake_profile_login.py -q
pytest tests/test_wake_smoke_state.py -q
```

Root pytest configuration must add the workspace root to `pythonpath` so `experimental.advisors` resolves under the console-script `pytest` entrypoint. `tests/test_stable_wake_browser_adapter.py` must unit-test version-controlled `scripts/stable_wake_browser_adapter.py`, **not** assume that the separately installed `.catdesk/wake-bridge/stable-runtime-v1` exists. The installed adapter's provenance, SHA, presence, and browser acceptance remain independent release gates: passing source tests does **not** attest or install that runtime. A 30-second `verify_project` timeout is not equivalent to a test failure; the receipt test subset alone can take over 20 seconds. Preserve full-suite verification status separately from focused results.
## Current read-only USER mismatch codes

The existing `operator web rollover inspect-quarantine --project-id PROJECT
--expected-intent-id INTENT` reads the active saved candidate. These compact
refusal codes carry no message text or recovery authority:

| Code | Observation |
| --- | --- |
| `RESPONSE_EXPECTED_USER_NOT_FOUND` | No exact message or trusted full-message anchor; no complete matching bootstrap header was verified. |
| `RESPONSE_EXPECTED_USER_DUPLICATED` | Multiple exact USER digests are present. |
| `RESPONSE_USER_REPRESENTATION_MISMATCH` | Trusted anchors see the expected text while the dedicated USER reader does not. |
| `RESPONSE_EXPECTED_USER_BODY_MISMATCH` | The latest USER has the complete expected bootstrap header, but its full normalized body differs. |
| `RESPONSE_USER_VIEW_CHANGED` | The additional private read changed the digest list; the hint is discarded. |
| `RESPONSE_USER_DOCUMENT_NOT_HYDRATED` | Both USER and anchor views remain empty at the existing bounded wait deadline. |

All preserve the quarantine and saved receipt/binding state. Body mismatch can
reflect altered or incomplete UI text; it does not establish its cause. Matching
only a header cannot authorize continuation. Target/query failures retain their
existing fixed refusal codes. Package/live evidence is recorded separately from
source fixtures in the parent dated audit.
